Could not reach: the running app (no execution, so nothing here is measured on a device — target sizes are read off CSS, not rendered); player/queue-manager.js (115KB) and queue-state.js internals, so I cannot speak to what happens at the very end of a Foray beyond `state.type === \\\"ended\\\"` producing no UI; the Capacitor shell's native audio plugin (mobile/plugins/foray-audio) beyond one grep, so the iOS lock-screen behaviour may differ from the web path I traced; search-engine.js and the whole #/shows search surface (deliberately deprioritised — typing is not a car activity); data/segments.json and discover.json copy (the `why` lines that fill every Foray row, and the hooks on episode cards) were only sampled, not read; docs/ entirely.",
 "where_i_would_quit": "The moment the first episode ends. I am in the middle lane, the audio stops, and nothing replaces it — no next episode, no message, no sound at all. I have to unlock the phone, open the app, and pick something while driving, and then it happens again forty minutes later. The founder's own stated reason for building this app is that he wants podcasts to keep playing in the car; a newcomer who never finds a drawer toggle called \"Up Next auto-advance\" gets the opposite of that, and after the second silence I would delete it and go back to Apple Podcasts.",
 "dropped_count": 9
}

 "verdict": "real_friction",
 "corrected_severity": "high",
 "apple_comparison_fair": true,
 "reason": "All three factual claims verified in C:\\Users\\wjduv\\Desktop\\Vibe Coding\\foray\\app.js. (1) `function autoAdvanceOn() { return lsGet(\"cp_autoadvance\", false); }` at app.js:1595 — default OFF. (2) `advanceQueueOnEnded` (app.js:1656-1685) bails exactly as described: `if (!autoAdvanceOn()) return;` (1660), `if (!wasFromQueue) return;` (1661), and at the end of list `// End of the queue: stop cleanly, no loop, no pulling in more content.` / `if (!nextId) return;` (1669-1670). (3) The origin stamp is set at only one call site: app.js:7028 `bindPlay($(\"#view\"), { origin: \"queue\" })` in renderQueue; the other eight bindPlay calls (3074, 3131, 5978, 6514, 6709, 6989, 7214) omit origin, and app.js:3731-3732 `if (origin === \"queue\") setQueuePlaybackOrigin(id); else clearQueuePlaybackOrigin();` means any play started from home, a show page, an episode page, a playlist or the library actively clears the origin, so audio stops dead at the end. (4) The switch is `drawerToggle(\"autoadvance-toggle\", \"Up Next auto-advance\", autoAdvanceOn, ...)` at app.js:9379, rendered by paintDrawerToggles as `\"<label>: off\"` (default `words = [\"off\",\"on\"]`, app.js:9330) against a bare `<button id=\"autoadvance-toggle\">` at index.html:58. Severity is low, not high: worst case is audio ends and the listener re-taps \"Jump back in\", with the position intact.\n\nAPPLE COMPARISON — NOT FAIR. I cannot substantiate any Apple Podcasts behaviour from this repo, and I will not take it on faith. The narrow fact (Apple's Now Playing carries no \"Stop\") is plausible, but the inference drawn from it does not hold. Apple does expose stop — via the OS media surface — and 4a exposes it there too (`episodeMediaSurface.stop` / `forayMediaSurface.stop`, client.js:1520 and :1533). Apple's in-app dismissal is an undiscoverable swipe, which is precisely the gesture-hidden pattern client.js:521-522 rejects as unsafe in a car. Citing \"no stop button in the player\" while omitting both of those makes the comparison misleading rather than a fair benchmark."

It is `return type === \"playing\" || type === \"transitioning\";` (client.js:744-745), not `=== \"playing\"`. Conclusion unaffected.\n\nCORRECTION 2 — `render()` does NOT paint from `isPlaying()`. A stalled element is NOT `paused` — so during a stall BOTH sides of that OR are true. The #689 reconciliation machinery built precisely to stop the transport lying is structurally incapable of catching this: it is an OR that only ever WIDENS toward \"running\". `reconcileWithBackend` is documented to catch the lie \"in one direction only\" (queue-manager.js:979-981). So a fix cannot be threaded through the existing composition; it needs a third, NARROWING input (a stall flag set on `waiting`/`stalled` and cleared on `playing`/`timeupdate` advancing). A stall AT A SEAM therefore does show \"Loading…\". A mid-playback stall does not, because `s.playing` is true and `running` short-circuits first.\n- Load-time stalls are bounded: `LOAD_SETTLE_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000` / `LOAD_SETTLE_TIMEOUT_HIDDEN_MS = 20_000` (html-audio-backend.js:170, 258), whose comment says a stalled fetch \"fires `stalled` and `suspend` and then nothing at all, forever\" and that without a deadline you get \"a listener stranded mid-Foray with a UI that still says loading\". The seam-beat exemption is real but explicitly scoped: calling the authored 2.0 s beat \"Loading…\" \"would be the app apologising for a silence it chose on purpose\" (client.js:918-921, app.js:8965-8968). A network stall is not a chosen silence, and the code distinguishes the two by name.  "where_i_would_quit": "First tap of the first episode I actually want. I press ▶ on a row; the button fills with the accent colour instantly — because `:hover` and `[data-playing=\"1\"]` are the same declaration (styles.css:1164/1165) and a phone tap leaves a sticky hover — and the mini bar slides up with the title, because `setNowPlaying` runs before the await (player/client.js:2120). The enclosure is dead, `play()` returns false, `bindPlay` drops it on the floor (app.js:3734), and the player module has no error string for an episode anywhere — `ui.note` can only ever say \"Timings on this show are approximate.\" (player/client.js:1165). I tap again — and the button is now showing ❚❚, so I think I'm pausing, when in fact I'm re-entering `play()` and seeking (player/html-audio-backend.js:1487). Two taps, three lies, silence. Rendered into the Foray page subtitle at app.js:8087 (`<p class=\"sub\">${esc(forayHeadSub(r))}</p>`).\n- app.js:8109 and :8111 — `aria-label=\"Previous segment\"` / `aria-label=\"Next segment\"`. Duplicated in C:\\Users\\wjduv\\Desktop\\Vibe Coding\\foray\\player\\client.js:2848-2849 inside `setSkipButtonMode`, which swaps the ±15/30s labels for these two inside a Foray. These are the hands-free path, so the persona hears them.\n- app.js:8124 — `` `${lost} segment${lost === 1 ? \"\" : \"s\"} can't play — listed below.` ``\n- app.js:9391 — `drawerToggle(\"interlude-toggle\", \"Jingle between segments\", ...)`.\n- data\\forays.json:284 — `\"title\": \"Barbecue: eight beats of a forty-beat history\"`, rendered raw via `<h2>${esc(r.title)}</h2>` (app.js:8086). \"beat\" is unambiguously the pipeline unit: the same record carries `beats_total: 40` and `beats_represented`, and item ids read `narration-act-1-2-beat`.\nSo: \"segment\" reaches listeners on at least five surfaces, two of them accessible names.\n\nCORRECTION 1 — the \"part 12 of 32\" evidence is a MISREAD. player\\client.js:903 builds `` show: `${show} · part ${index + 1} of ${total}` ``, and the comment above it (client.js:895-897) shows \"part\" was chosen deliberately as the listener word: \"the part number because a Foray's second line has room and 'part 12 of 32' is the one fact a mini bar can add.\" \"Part\" is plain English, not pipeline vocabulary — the pipeline words are \"segment\" and \"beat\". Drop this item from the fix plan. But it yields the strongest real finding the persona did not name: the SAME player words one unit two ways — the mini bar says \"part 12 of 32\" while its own transport buttons say \"Previous segment\" / \"Next segment\" (client.js:2848-2849). That internal inconsistency, not the word \"segment\" alone, is the defensible core of the ticket, and \"part\" is the in-repo precedent for the fix.\n\nCORRECTION 2 — the \"beats\" title is DRAFT-GATED, not listener-facing by default. `grilling-history-2` is `\"status\": \"draft\"`; drafts are filtered out unless `showDraftsOn()` (app.js:7421 `lsGet(\"cp_show_drafts\", false)`, applied at app.js:7485) or an explicit `?foray=` unlock. Of 8 records in data\\forays.json exactly one is published (`capital-types-1`, \"The types of capital a startup can raise\"). So a newcomer cannot normally see \"beats\"; it still needs fixing before that Foray publishes, but it is not shipped copy today. Lower its priority below the \"segment\" strings.\n\nNOT a deliberate_choice: no CLAUDE.md principle and no decision record chooses \"segment\" as the listener word. Grepping DECISIONS.md, CLAUDE.md, docs\\marketing\\09-product-feature-review.md and the test suite turns up \"segment\" only as pipeline/ADR/schema vocabulary (ADR-0007, tools/segments/, data/segments.json, `type: \"segment\"`). Tellingly, the design reference docs\\ux\\foray-m3-prototype.html contains ZERO occurrences of \"segment\" in any casing — the mockup never used the word. It leaked from the data model into the UI by default. Avoidable, so real_friction.\n\nHARD CONSTRAINT the fix plan MUST respect: docs\\DECISIONS.md (2026-08-11 playback ruling) forbids one of the persona's three proposed replacements. A Foray does seek-and-stop against the publisher's original enclosure and never produces a derived audio file; the entry rejects a mockup that \"described the feature as 'clipping' and 'stitching'\" and rules \"Copy must follow the mechanism: no user-facing language implying we produce a new audio file.\" So \"clips\" is off the table on legal-posture grounds — do not let a copy pass introduce it. Also note copy in `data/` is gated by a CI copy-rules test (backend/test/copyRules.test.ts, per docs\\marketing\\09-product-feature-review.md R23), so any new vocabulary should land there rather than only in templates.\n\nAPPLE COMPARISON — false, partially substantiated at best. \"Episodes\" is right, and Apple Podcasts does surface publisher-supplied \"Chapters\" (4a investigated and rejected `<podcast:chapters>` for its own reasons — DECISIONS.md 2026-08-11: only 10 of 209 probed feeds publish them). But I cannot substantiate that Apple Podcasts uses \"clips\" as user-facing vocabulary, and I am not asserting it does. More importantly the comparison supplies no usable alternative: Apple Podcasts has no construct equivalent to a Foray — a timed sequence assembled across multiple shows — so it has no word for this unit to borrow. And the persona's framing that \"nothing user-facing should carry the words the build pipeline uses internally\" does not survive its own example: \"episode\" and \"chapter\" are themselves RSS/ID3 publishing-pipeline terms that Apple shows users. The test is whether a word is comprehensible to a newcomer, not whether it also appears in the codebase.\n\nFIX PLAN SHOULD: (1) pick one listener-facing unit word and use it everywhere in the player — \"part\" already has an in-repo rationale and passes the legal constraint; (2) fix the two accessible names in BOTH places (app.js:8109/8111 and player\\client.js:2848-2849) or they will diverge again; (3) update app.js:7990, :8124, :9391; (4) retitle data\\forays.json grilling-history-2 before it leaves draft. Do NOT adopt \"clips\"."

There is no listener-facing failure surface for an ordinary episode anywhere in the app. The player's one telemetry sink routes errors to a Foray and only to a Foray: player/client.js:2007 `if (foray && /player\\.error|segment\\.skipped\\.atLoad/i.test(m)) { foray.error = m; notifyForay(); }`. For a single episode `foray` is null (ForayPlayer.play sets `foray = null` before starting), so a media error or a refused `play()` reaches only `console.warn(\"[player]\", m)` and `diag.note(m)` (client.js:1992-1993). client.js says so itself at 1977-1984: \"a media error and a refused `play()` now also reach `console.warn`, which is an improvement on reaching nothing — and neither matches the narrower `foray.error` test below, so the Foray page's standing-error rule is untouched\". `ui.note` is confirmed to carry only \"\" or \"Timings on this show are approximate.\" (client.js:1165), and grep finds no error copy outside the Foray block — `FY_START_FAILED`/`FY_AUTOPLAY_HINT`/`FY_TAP_FAILED` (app.js:8525-8530) and `paintForayFailure` → `#fy-error` are the app's only failure lines. bindPlay (app.js:3723-3748) has no try/catch, as claimed.\n\nWHAT IS WRONG, AND IT MATTERS FOR THE FIX. (1) The quoted `if (!ok) { clearQueuePlaybackOrigin(); return; }` is near-unreachable. `ForayPlayer.play` returns false only when `canPlay(item)` is false, i.e. no `audio_url` (client.js:2090, 2083-2085), and `playBtn` refuses to render a button at all without `audio_url` (app.js:717-720: `if (!item || !item.audio_url) return \"\";`), with `notPlayableNote()` filling the slot instead (app.js:6560-6561, 6981). The only way to reach `!ok` is the partial-snapshot race app.js:3696-3704 already documents. (2) The REAL silent path is the opposite of a false return: a load, network or autoplay failure is caught inside the manager — player/queue-manager.js:1253-1260 `catch (err) { … await this._handle(E.error(...)) }` → idle + pause — so `await manager.play(0)` RESOLVES, `ForayPlayer.play` returns **true**, bindPlay proceeds to `logEvent(\"play_started\", …)`, and client.js `setNowPlaying` has already unhidden the mini bar (client.js:1107-1122) so the listener gets a bar showing the episode title at 0:00 with `render()` painting \"▶\" (client.js render, `transportIsRunning()`). That is worse than silence: it looks armed and started. (3) The unhandled-rejection claim is real but secondary — `_perform`'s `default: throw new Error(\"unhandled PlayerEffect\")` (queue-manager.js:1111), a `media.setActions` TypeError, or app.js/client.js service-worker vintage skew can still escape both unguarded async functions.\n\nNOT A DELIBERATE CHOICE. No CLAUDE.md principle asks for this. \"State observed, never declared\" argues the other way, and the app's own precedent is founder report #225, whose whole premise (app.js:8548-8562) is that \"a tap that does nothing and says nothing\" is a defect; `guardForayStart`/`guardForayTap` were built for exactly this and were simply never extended past the Foray page. Nothing in docs/DECISIONS.md, STATE.md or the code records a decision to keep episode failures quiet — client.js:1982 only protects the Foray page from FALSE standing errors, which is a different argument.\n\nNOT ALREADY HANDLED ELSEWHERE. Checked every other play call site: `advanceQueueOnEnded` (app.js:1678-1683) has the same silent `!ok` shape plus no `.catch`; the timestamp control (app.js:6941-6950) does wrap in try/catch but swallows it silently (and passes `\"timestamp\"` as the options argument where `{ why }` is expected — a separate minor bug worth a line in the plan). No toast mechanism exists in app.js (the only hit is app.js:1591 calling one \"out of scope for Stage 1\").\n\nSEVERITY medium, not high: the mini bar and the ▶ glyph are some observable state, and the common failure needs a dead enclosure, a dropped connection or an autoplay refusal. It stays real for the hands-free case because the autoplay refusal has ready-made copy on the Foray page (\"press play again and it will start\") that the mini player never shows.\n\nNo Apple Podcasts comparison was made — the persona compared 4a to 4a's own Foray page — so apple_comparison_fair is left unset rather than answered about a claim nobody made.\n\nAUDIT ONLY: no files were changed."
}

======== journal line 64 | result #29 | phase=Check | label=check:impatient:Nothing in the app explains | agent=aefb3c16d9f589b3b
{
 "corrected_severity": "medium",
 "verdict": "real_friction",
 "reason": "FACTUALLY CONFIRMED, with two omissions that narrow it slightly but do not defeat it.\n\nConfirmed: app.js:4119 (\"We clip the best parts of several podcasts on a subject and stitch them into one seamless listen, with a narrator bridging the gaps.\") is the only sentence in the shipped product that states what a Foray IS, and it is unreachable after one visit. `showFirstTimeExplainerOnce()` (app.js:4038) returns early on `if (!isGenuineFirstTimeUser()) return false;` (4039) and `if (lsGet(\"cp_intro_dismissed\", false)) return false;` (4040); `dismiss` sets `lsSet(\"cp_intro_dismissed\", true)` (4063) and is wired to both `scrim.addEventListener(\"click\", dismiss)` (4067) and the \"Skip for now\" button (4132). The legacy `showIntroPopupOnce()` (app.js:4230) shares that same flag and its copy is about the four topic queues (\"Grouped into four topic queues…\", 4254), not Forays. There is no about/help/how-it-works surface: the drawer is five bare labels including `<a class=\"drawer-section\" href=\"#/forays\">Forays</a>` (index.html:52, pinned at five by test/home-information-architecture.test.js); `renderForays()` paints `<h2>Forays</h2>` + `${list.length} foray${...}` and then rows of `<span class=\"fy-home-kicker\">foray…</span>` + title only (app.js:6533-6537, 9113-9119); `forayCardV2Html` on Home is title + colour strip + an optional stretch bridge line (app.js:6389-6394); `forayHeadSub` is \"N segments · N shows · clock\" (app.js:7988-7996).\n\nMISSED BY THE PERSONA (both worth knowing for the fix, neither a defence): (1) the Foray page DOES render a description — `${r.foray.summary ? `<p class=\"fy-summary\">${esc(r.foray.summary)}</p>` : \"\"}` (app.js:8091) — and all 8 Forays in data/forays.json carry one, but every summary describes the SUBJECT (\"Eight ways to fund a company, from an in-law's cheque to an SBA loan.\"), never the format, so it does not answer \"what is this thing\". (2) The Forays empty state does carry format language — \"No forays right now — 4a stitches these by hand, so they arrive a few at a time.\" (app.js:6545) — but it renders only when the list is empty, i.e. never beside a listed Foray. The remaining format signals are inferable, not explanatory, and all sit past the decision point: \"Where this came from\" with \"Every clip plays from the show's own feed, so the download counts for them\" (app.js:7975-7977) is below the whole running order; the `AI Narrator` credit (app.js:7651) is a per-row byline; the show-page footnote \"4a stitched a clip from it into these\" (app.js:2792) is on a different screen.\n\nNOT A DELIBERATE CHOICE FROM A PRINCIPLE. CLAUDE.md's product principles (§1 exploration floor / no engagement dark patterns, §2 state observed never declared, §3 legally boring, §4 copy rules) say nothing that requires the format to go unnamed; §2 governs config and learned parameters, not product explanation. docs/ui-transition-plan.md:131 deliberately makes the first-run sheet \"skippable at every step\" gated on `cp_intro_dismissed` — that skip is intentional, but no doc makes that sheet the sole home for the definition. Two real constraints shape the FIX rather than justify the friction: CLAUDE.md §4 copy rules (hooks ≤16 words, why-lines ≤18, no \"deep dive\"/clickbait withholding) cap the length, and test/home-information-architecture.test.js pins the drawer at five destinations and Home at four subject cards, so a standing line belongs on #/forays (under the \"N forays\" sub) and/or on the Foray page beside `fy-summary` — not as a new Home row or drawer item.\n\nNO APPLE PODCASTS COMPARISON was made in this complaint, so apple_comparison_fair is left unset rather than guessed; nothing here rests on a claim about another app.\n\nSEVERITY medium: it is the app's most alien concept versus any ordinary podcast app and the fix is one short standing line in two templates, but a newcomer is not left with zero information (title, subject summary, segment strip, \"N segments · N shows · 1:02:11\", and the sources block), so the cost is a hesitant first tap rather than a broken or misleading flow."
}

{
 "verdict": "real_friction",

 "reason": "FACTUAL CHECK — substantially correct, with one small inaccuracy in their quote of the handler.\n\nThe button is global and unlabelled. index.html:37 `<button id=\"refresh-btn\" aria-label=\"New suggestions\">↻</button>` sits in the static `<header class=\"topbar\">`, markup outside `#view`, so it is present on every route. Nothing hides or relabels it per route: `grep -n refresh styles.css` returns nothing, and `grep -n topbar app.js` returns exactly one hit (app.js:11647, an unrelated comment about a banner positioned below the topbar). There is no `title=` attribute anywhere in index.html, so the only statement of intent — \"New suggestions\" — reaches screen readers and nobody else. A sighted newcomer sees ↻ and nothing more.\n\nThe handler, app.js:11492-11497, verbatim:\n    $(\"#refresh-btn\").addEventListener(\"click\", () => {\n      buildCards();\n      logEvent(\"refreshed_all\", {});\n      if ((location.hash || \"#/\") === \"#/\") renderHome();\n      else location.hash = \"#/\";\n    });\nThe persona wrote that it \"calls buildCards(), logs refreshed_all, and then location.hash = '#/'\" — they missed the same-hash branch. That branch only fires when you are already on Home (re-render in place, no navigation). On every other route the `else` fires and you are navigated to Home, which is precisely the complaint. The missed branch does not rescue the app: the complained-of behaviour is real on every screen except the one where the button makes sense.\n\nNOT ALREADY HANDLED. There is no other way to get new suggestions — `grep -i \"shuffle|new suggestions|re-roll|reroll\" app.js` finds only internal helpers (`seededShuffle`, app.js:4342) and comments, no second control. Nor is there a route-scoped refresh the ↻ could be mistaken for: no pull-to-refresh exists (`grep -i \"pull-to-refresh|pulltorefresh|touchmove\" app.js` returns nothing), no long-press, no per-page reload. So ↻ is both the only refresh affordance in the app and not a refresh of anything the user is looking at.\n\nNOT A PRINCIPLE. The exploration floor does live in buildCards — its header (app.js:1059-1067) explains the 4-slot menu keeps \"one slot… a structural exploration floor\" per CLAUDE.md product principle #1. But that principle governs WHAT buildCards picks, not where its trigger lives or which glyph fires it. Nothing in CLAUDE.md speaks to the topbar (`grep -i \"refresh|suggestions|topbar\" CLAUDE.md` hits only the nightly content pipeline under tools/refresh/). Telling evidence: this codebase comments its deliberate decisions at length — the very next block, app.js:11498-11507, spends nine lines and a measured scroll offset justifying `history.scrollRestoration = \"manual\"`. The refresh handler carries no comment at all, and no test pins its behaviour (the ~35 test hits for \"refresh-btn\" are all id-inventory lists in DOM stubs, e.g. test/home-information-architecture.test.js:75, not assertions about clicking it). An undefended, uncommented, untested cross-route navigation is incidental, not a stated choice.\n\nWHY MEDIUM, NOT HIGH. `location.hash = \"#/\"` pushes a history entry, so browser back returns you, and route() restores remembered scroll (the app.js:11498 comment documents a back-step to `#/shows` landing at the remembered 4000). Nothing is destroyed, no data is lost, no playback is interrupted. The only irreversible part is small: buildCards() reshuffles Home's four cards before navigating, so the set you had is gone — back returns you to your page but not to your old suggestions. WHY NOT LOW: it is one of only two buttons in the permanent chrome, present on every screen, visually unlabelled, and its effect is off-screen navigation — the worst combination for a misclick, and a newcomer reaching for ↻ to re-fetch a stalled episode list will hit it early and often.\n\nFix framing is placement/labelling, not removal: the control is legitimate on Home and homeless everywhere else.\n\nAPPLE COMPARISON: none was made, so I left apple_comparison_fair unset rather than answer a question the persona did not ask. Their claim is about the glyph in general — \"the most universally-understood glyph in software\" — not about Apple Podcasts' behaviour. I cannot verify Apple Podcasts' current header controls from this repo and will not assert them."
}

Principle #1 (\"Curiosity/learning first; anti-echo-chamber. The empty state (app.js:1031) is \"No starred shows yet — star a show from its page to see it here.\" — \"see it here\" is the *only* hint, and it describes the list, not the absence of a feed. ======== journal line 108 | result #47 | phase=Check | label=check:first-launch:Continuous playback — the th | agent=aa3cf4f96a5b495ed
{
 "verdict": "real_friction",
 "corrected_severity": "high",
 "apple_comparison_fair": true,
 "reason": "FACTUALLY CORRECT on every point. app.js:1595 `function autoAdvanceOn() { return lsGet(\"cp_autoadvance\", false); }` — default false. advanceQueueOnEnded (app.js:1655-1685) adds `if (!autoAdvanceOn()) return; if (!wasFromQueue) return;`, origin is stamped only by renderQueue's `bindPlay($(\"#view\"), { origin: \"queue\" })` (app.js:7028), and the end of list is `// End of the queue: stop cleanly, no loop, no pulling in more content. if (!nextId) return;`. The sole surface is the drawer row `drawerToggle(\"autoadvance-toggle\", \"Up Next auto-advance\", ...)` (app.js:9379). Nothing anywhere else: grep for autoadvance/auto-advance across app.js returns only these call sites, and no long-press, menu, or per-episode variant exists. NOT already_handled.\n\nNOT deliberate_choice, and this is the crux the persona could not see. The app.js comment block at 1571-1594 justifies every gate by citing a rule that no longer exists: \"CLAUDE.md product principle #1 explicitly bans 'autoplay chains' as a dark pattern. This feature is a genuine, deliberate exception carved out of that rule.\" That clause was STRUCK eight days before this audit. docs/DECISIONS.md:2591 is titled `## 2026-09-14 (continuous playback is wanted: the \"no autoplay chains\" clause is struck)` and says: \"That clause is struck. Continuous playback is now an explicitly wanted behaviour: when an episode ends, keep playing — the rest of the list the listener started from, and then more of what fits.\" So the code's stated rationale is a stale citation of a deleted rule, and the DEFAULT OFF / queue-only / no-continuation design is not downstream of any live principle.\n\nThe ruling went further and pre-rejected the exact defense this complaint would get. DECISIONS.md:2622-2630 measures the current state by name — \"Auto-advance already existed and was well built... It was reachable from exactly one surface and was off by default\" — and concludes verbatim: \"**The founder was asking for a default, not a feature.**\" It also names the narrow reading (\"advance within a finite list the listener chose, never extend it\") as \"what this session first proposed\" and records that \"The founder rejected that reading explicitly. It is recorded here because it is the reading a future agent will reach for, and the ruling is that it is not enough.\" The `if (!nextId) return;` stop and the queue-origin gate are precisely that rejected reading, still shipping.\n\nThe only thing standing between the ruling and the code is issue #691 (DECISIONS.md:2647, \"whose scope this ruling widens from 'walk the chosen list' to 'keep playing'\"), which has no STATE.md workstream. CLAUDE.md's own meta-lesson forbids treating that as cover: \"a default is not a setting and an open ticket is not a state... Read the live value.\" The live value is false.\n\nSeverity high, not medium: this is the founder's one named use case (the car), the governing document promises the behavior in the present tense, and the fix the ruling asks for is a default flip plus removing two gates — cheap, and currently blocking the product's stated purpose. Note for the planner: DECISIONS.md:2637-2646 constrains the fix — a Foray must stay untouched (the player deliberately never reports `ended` for one, player/client.js:686), `cp_autoadvance` must survive as an off-switch, and a browser-refused chained play must fail visibly rather than look like the end of the list.\n\nAPPLE COMPARISON FAIR: yes. Apple Podcasts ships a Continuous Playback setting that is on by default and walks Up Next without the listener touching anything, so \"as a newcomer I expect what Apple Podcasts does\" is a sound baseline, and 4a's Up Next is the direct analogue of Apple's queue. I am asserting Apple's default from general product knowledge, not from anything in this repo, and cannot verify it here. One honest qualification that does NOT weaken the complaint: Apple also stops when its queue is exhausted rather than pulling in recommendations, so the \"then more of what fits\" half is 4a's own higher bar set by the 2026-09-14 founder ruling, not an Apple parity gap. (3) File sizes confirmed on disk: discover.json 2457.7 KB, item-tags.json 415.7 KB, segments.json 200.1 KB, forays.json 127.3 KB, catalog-client.json 97.9 KB, segment-sources.json 72.2 KB, semantic-index.json 60.2 KB, taxonomy.json 47.5 KB, session.json 22.5 KB, validated-links.json 15.2 KB — and `tools/web/prepare-dist.mjs:62` lists \"discover.json\" in the dist allowlist, so the 2.4 MB file ships untrimmed. (4) `grep -i \"loading|spinner|skeleton|shimmer|placeholder|pulse\" styles.css` returns only `styles.css:707` (a comment about `#pl-form button:disabled`) and `styles.css:2747` `.voice-loading` — there is no boot loading style. (5) First-open coldness is real: `sw.js` precaches in `install` (sw.js:160-169) and `vercel.json` sets `/data/(.*)` to `max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so on the very first open no cache can serve these.\n\nNOT a deliberate choice. I read CLAUDE.md:236-255 in full. The four product principles are exploration floor / no dark patterns, state-observed-never-declared, legally-boring, and copy rules. None of them implies or requires a blank first paint; `grep -i \"boot|first paint|skeleton|spinner|loading\" CLAUDE.md` returns nothing. The blank boot is an omission, not a stance.\n\nNOT already handled. Two nuances the fix planner should have, both of which cut toward \"avoidable\":\n  - The codebase already owns this idiom for in-app navigation and just never applied it to boot: `app.js:8004` `$(\"#view\").innerHTML = '<div class=\"page\"><p class=\"note\">Loading…</p></div>'` in `renderForay()`, and `app.js:2957` `view.innerHTML = '...Loading show…...'` on the show route. So this is an inconsistency with the app's own established pattern, not a missing capability.\n  - `init()` already writes copy into `#view` on the FAILURE path (`app.js:11360`, \"Couldn't load 4a — check your connection and reload.\") — the app has words for \"this went wrong\" and none for \"this is working.\"\n  - The blank window has two phases: the first await is only session.json (22.5 KB) plus one IndexedDB read, so a boot state could be painted very cheaply either synchronously in index.html or immediately after that first small fetch, well before the 2.4 MB discover.json lands.\n\nSeverity corrected to medium, not high. The screen is genuinely bare with zero text or affordance during a cold multi-megabyte fetch, and it lands on the single worst moment (first open, first impression). But it is self-resolving, first-open-only (the service worker covers subsequent launches), costs no data or functionality, and the wire transfer is smaller than the 3.5 MB on-disk figure since Vercel gzip/brotli-compresses JSON — the persona's byte count is honest about disk but overstates the wall-clock duration.\n\napple_comparison_fair: true, with one caveat I could not substantiate from inside this repo. I cannot run Apple Podcasts, so I am not accepting the specific \"Listen Now skeleton tiles\" detail on faith — on a genuine post-install first launch with no followed shows, Apple Podcasts' Listen Now is closer to a sparse empty state with a prompt than to shimmering placeholders being filled in, so that half is somewhat idealized. The load-bearing half of the comparison is fair and needs no external verification: the expectation that a mainstream podcast app never presents a content area containing literally nothing — no text, no placeholder, no progress affordance — for the duration of a cold multi-megabyte fetch is a correct and conventional baseline, and 4a's own show and foray routes hold themselves to it."
}

 "dropped_count": 7,
 "where_i_would_quit": "Twenty minutes into a Foray in the car, I miss a sentence and tap the button that is back-15-seconds in every other podcast app I have used. Inside a Foray it is silently `‹‹` (player/client.js:2846) and, because I am more than 4 seconds into the clip (`RESTART_WINDOW_SEC = 4`, player/client.js:2807, 2834), it restarts the clip from the beginning — losing ten minutes of listening with no undo and no way to scrub while driving. Nothing warned me the button changed meaning, and there is no small-seek control anywhere inside a Foray. That is not \"a different product\"; that is the app punishing me for the most ordinary action there is. I delete it at the next red light.",
 "coverage_note": "READ IN FULL OR IN THE RELEVANT PART: index.html (all 68 lines); app.js — every heading, `class=\\\"note\\\"`, `class=\\\"sub\\\"`, placeholder, aria-label and `<h2>/<h3>` (grepped exhaustively), plus full reads of the first-run explainer (3843-4130), Home v2 (6110-6500), the Forays list and Foray detail pages (6505-6560, 7795-8140), Create (7255-7350), search result rendering (5547-6110), the drawer and its ten controls (9185-9470, 10320-10545), the router (10586-10790) and the Interests page (500-560); player/client.js labels, transport, mini-player and voice sheet; player/media-session.js (lock-screen / car metadata in full); player/segment-strip.js `stripSummary`; player/foray-resolve.js drop/skip reasons; player/foray-progress.js `remainingLabel`; player/interlude.js header; styles.css for every badge, tag and dim-text rule I cite. DATA: data/forays.json parsed in full (all 8 Forays — titles, summaries, slot titles, per-item why-lines and all narration scripts), data/taxonomy.json node ids/labels, data/discover.json's 2,167 hooks scanned for the jargon list and the CLAUDE.md banned words (clean — every \\\"beat\\\"/\\\"segment\\\"/\\\"stretch\\\" hit there is ordinary English).\\n\\nCOULD NOT REACH: I cannot run the app, so everything about rendered layout, tap targets and timing is inferred from code and CSS, not observed. I did not read search-engine.js (141 KB), sw.js, api/, backend/, mobile/ or ios/ — a user-visible string could hide in the service worker's offline copy or the native shell. data/segments.json and data/segment-sources.json I sampled through the published Foray only, so I have not checked every why-line in the corpus for jargon. I did not open docs/ux/foray-m3-prototype.html, so where shipped copy diverges from the design reference I could not say which is intended. I also did not audit the episode detail page (`renderEpisode`, app.js:6956) or the Library/Queue pages line-by-line — a skim of their headings and empty states found them plain, but that is a skim.\\n\\nONE THING I DELIBERATELY DID NOT FILE: that Forays themselves are unfamiliar. The first-run sheet explains them well — \\\"Forays: one subject, many shows … We clip the best parts of several podcasts on a subject and stitch them into one seamless listen, with a narrator bridging the gaps\\\" (app.js:4111-4114) — which is clearer than anything else in the app. Its weakness is that it shows once, is dismissible, and is never restated: the Foray detail page's only subtitle is \\\"22 segments · 5 shows · 1h 4m\\\" (app.js:7986-7993). That is in my dropped set, not a filed finding.\\n\\nDROPPED (7 real candidates, all grounded): (1) the `↻` topbar button, aria-label \\\"New suggestions\\\" (index.html:37), re-deals Home's picks irrecoverably via `buildCards()` and also yanks you to Home from whatever page you were on (app.js:11492-11497); (2) the Create tab ships a permanently disabled \\\"Foray\\\" toggle with `title=\\\"Custom Forays aren't available yet\\\"` (app.js:7281) — a dead control on a main tab; (3) playlist search rows say \\\"N parts\\\" (app.js:5602) where every other playlist surface says \\\"N episodes\\\" (app.js:6691, 6440); (4) `.hv2-draft-tag` and `.hv2-stretch-tag` are byte-identical in size, weight and colour (styles.css:3228, 3257), so DRAFT and STRETCH are indistinguishable at a glance; (5) the AI narrator is labelled \\\"AI Narrator\\\" on the Foray page (app.js:7678) but nowhere in the mini bar, the expanded player or the lock screen — in the car a synthetic voice arrives with the screen reading \\\"Up next: <episode>\\\" and no attribution (player/media-session.js:402-404); (6) the \\\"Open in\\\" toggle's two states are \\\"Apple Podcasts\\\" / \\\"Pocket Casts (show page)\\\" (app.js:9374-9377) with nothing saying what gets opened in them; (7) the Foray page never restates what a Foray is once onboarding is dismissed, per the paragraph above."
}

======== journal line 138 | result #62 | phase=Check | label=check:jargon:The voice picker says "Audit | agent=a9ae6f38bc502ea71
{
 "verdict": "real_friction",
 "corrected_severity": "medium",
 "apple_comparison_fair": true,
 "reason": "Every factual claim verifies. app.js:10127 carries the quoted sub-copy verbatim; app.js:10168 builds the button as ddEl(\"button\",\"voice-row-audition\", ... : \"Audition\"); app.js:10251 reads \"None of 4a's trial voices are installed here.\" Grep over app.js, player/ and styles.css finds no \"Preview\" or \"Play sample\" anywhere — \"Audition\" is the only label for this action on any screen, so nothing is hidden elsewhere. Not already_handled and not misread.\n\nNot deliberate_choice either: no CLAUDE.md principle produces this wording. The product principles (CLAUDE.md:236-256) are the exploration floor / no dark patterns, state-observed-never-declared, legally boring, and copy rules (why-lines <= 18 words, hooks <= 16, banned words) — none touch these labels. By contrast the genuinely principled decisions in this sheet are all commented and defended in code (no Open Settings button because @capacitor/app exposes no such method; the curated VOICE_ALLOWLIST; voiceQualityLabel refusing to invent a tier word). The jargon simply was never examined. \"Audition\" is a casting term, \"trial voices\" collides with paid-trial in an app context (the shortlist is not a trial of anything), and \"Greyed\"/\"fetch\" both need translating.\n\nCarve-out for the fix plan: the ten-count sample itself is deliberate and must not change — app.js:10087 records the founder cutting it from twenty counts with spoken markers on 2026-09-10 (\"reduce the script to just counting to ten, it was so bad listening to them for so long\"), and HUMAN-ACTIONS H3 still takes its stopwatch reading against that line. Only the sentence describing it is in scope; the code also deliberately refuses to state a seconds count, which any rewrite must preserve.\n\nExtra defect the persona missed, same sentence: paintVoiceList (app.js:10238) emits greyed rows only when voiceState.path === \"native\", but the \"Greyed voices are free downloads\" sentence is static sheet copy — on the Web Speech path the sheet explains a row kind that cannot appear there.\n\nScope/cost: copy is pinned by tests — test/voice-settings.test.js:466 asserts /trial voices/, :503 asserts the sub says /count to ten/, and :320/:327/:339 describe rows in terms of \"Audition\"; styles.css:2736 .voice-row-audition is a class name and can stay.\n\nSeverity medium, not high: it is a settings sheet rather than a core listening flow, and no data is lost — but it is a first-run comprehension failure at the exact moment a newcomer is deciding, and \"trial voices\" risks implying a paywall in an app that has none, which is worth more than a pure style nit.\n\napple_comparison_fair = true, with a caveat the fix plan should honor: the persona compared to OS voice pickers (not Apple Podcasts, which has no voice picker). The thrust — that consumer OS pickers use a plain play/preview affordance and never a casting term — is fair. I could not substantiate the literal claim that iOS's own picker says \"Preview\": iOS Settings > Accessibility > Spoken Content > Voices uses an unlabeled play glyph on each row. So take plainness from the comparison, not the exact word."
}

Nothing visible explains the mode change — only the aria-labels (\"Previous segment\"/\"Next segment\") say it, so screen-reader users are told and sighted users are not.\n\nWHAT IS FALSE — \"Nothing anywhere in a Foray offers a small seek.\" Two places do.\n(1) The car dash, the surface the persona names as their muscle memory, is NOT affected. `forayMediaSurface` at client.js:1513-1525 includes `seekBy: (offset) => ForayPlayer.foraySeek(Math.max(0, forayPosition() + offset))`, installed by `media.setActions(forayMediaSurface)` at client.js:2585. media-session.js:531-534 turns that into `seekbackward`/`seekforward` at SEEK_BACKWARD_SEC=15 / SEEK_FORWARD_SEC=30 (media-session.js:212-213), honouring a head unit's own offset. So on the lock screen, CarPlay/Android Auto and the headphone pinch, back-15/forward-30 works inside a Foray, on the Foray's own clock, crossing segment seams. Only the in-app buttons swap.\n(2) The persona missed the control directly above the buttons they audited. `#fy-strip` is a scrubber, not decoration — app.js:8755 \"The strip is a scrubber, not 32 buttons\"; the click handler (app.js:8770-8783) reads the coordinate FIRST and calls `player.foraySeek(at)`, so any point on it is that position in the hour. app.js:8309+ `bindStripZoomScrub` adds press-and-hold zoom-to-scrub with a floating iOS-magnifier bubble, built from live iPhone testing for exactly this case (\"thirty-two minutes in, 'back a bit' is the gesture people make\"). So \"the expanded player's scrub bar is the sole fine control\" is also wrong.\n\nNOT A PRINCIPLE. No CLAUDE.md product principle covers this (the four are exploration floor / state-observed / legally-boring / copy rules). The rationale is a design judgement stated only in a code comment at client.js:1765-1767 — \"a segment here is often under two minutes, so a 30-second nudge mostly leaves it anyway\" — which is sound but lives nowhere a user can see. That is why this is real_friction rather than deliberate_choice.\n\nRESIDUAL, AND SEVERITY. What survives is narrow: the two in-app tap targets flanking play silently change meaning with no visible cue, and a newcomer's reflex tap on ‹‹ eleven minutes into a clip costs them their place. Severity is medium, not high — the mistap is cheap to recover from because the scrubber sits inches above the button and the OS controls never changed. Treat this as a labelling/legibility problem (make the mode visible, make the strip read as the nudge) — NOT as \"add a 15s seek to a Foray,\" which would duplicate a control the app already has in three places.\n\nAPPLE COMPARISON — set false, for a scope reason, not a factual one. The persona's description of Apple Podcasts is accurate: its Now Playing screen does put ⟲15 / ⟳30 around play, and it never repurposes those two buttons. But the comparison is out of scope as applied. The user's own instruction excludes forays from the Apple-parity standard (\"too different from apps like Apple Podcasts (excluding forays of course)\"), and this complaint is entirely about inside-a-Foray behaviour. There is also no Apple counterpart to compare against — Apple Podcasts has no stitched-multi-source-clip mode, so there is no Apple behaviour 4a is deviating from here. Note too that 4a's restart-then-previous convention matches Apple's own prev-track behaviour, so the half of the design the persona found most surprising is the half that follows Apple most closely. Keep this finding on its own merits (an unexplained mode change), not on Apple parity."

I did not read mobile/ (the Capacitor shell), sw.js (service worker — so I cannot say what, if anything, is cached for offline beyond the app shell; if it caches audio, finding 1 would soften), search-engine.js, backend/, or the test suites except where a grep needed disambiguating. I also did not verify server-side episode ordering beyond confirming the endpoint paginates on published_at, so \"newest first\" on a show page is assumed from feed order rather than checked against live data.\n\nEight further real candidates left out, in rough order of cost: (1) a show page loads only the first 100 episodes (PAGE_SIZE = 100, api/shows/[show_id]/episodes.ts:145), the \"Show more episodes\" control was deliberately removed (app.js:3081-3084), and the subtitle is written to say nothing at all when the list is partial (app.js:2817-2820) — so a long-running show's back catalogue is silently unreachable; (2) the header's ↻ button (index.html:37, aria-label \"New suggestions\") navigates you to Home from wherever you are and re-deals the suggestion cards with no confirmation (app.js:11492-11497); (3) the tab bar is display:none'd while the search field has focus (styles.css:1435), so navigation vanishes mid-search; (4) the returning-user intro popup still describes a Home that was replaced — \"Grouped into four topic queues — not one long feed to scroll… Tap a card to open its queue\" (app.js:4248); (5) the Interests page is 41 percentage sliders (data/taxonomy.json has 41 roots) headed \"Drag a slider to overrule what 4a has learned\" (app.js:551), which sits awkwardly against \"state observed, never declared\"; (6) every episode row is numbered 1..N (`<span class=\"q-num\">`, app.js:6563) even on a show page, where a numbered list implies a ranking or running order that does not exist; (7) the feedback sheet's copy — \"What missed for you?\" and a submit button labelled \"Pick at least one\" (app.js:7862, 7869); (8) the Foray page carries its own full transport (‹‹ / ▶ Play / ›› / 1×, app.js:8110-8118) that duplicates and sits alongside the global mini-player, so two different play surfaces are on screen at once with different control sets."

======== journal line 152 | result #69 | phase=Check | label=check:jargon:The Interests page prints ra | agent=a4310bc0e18baa1e7
{
 "verdict": "real_friction",
 "corrected_severity": "medium",
 "apple_comparison_fair": true,
 "reason": "FACTUALLY CORRECT, but the complaint bundles two things with opposite verdicts. Keep them separate in the fix plan.\n\n(A) The raw id line — REAL FRICTION, the keeper. app.js:525 renders `<span class=\"interest-row-path\">${esc(node.id)}</span>` directly beneath `<span class=\"interest-row-name\">${esc(node.label)}</span>` (app.js:524), styled dim at styles.css:983 (`.interest-row-path { font-size: 0.72rem; color: var(--text-dim); }`). data/taxonomy.json confirms every one of the 194 nodes already carries a genuinely human `label` — `engineering/energy-fusion` => \"Fusion & energy systems\", `history/military-ancient` => \"Ancient military history\", `craft/instrument-making` => \"Instrument making\". So the id line is pure duplication of a label that is already good.\n\nNo CLAUDE.md product principle backs it. I checked all four (CLAUDE.md:237-253): exploration floor / no dark patterns, state-observed-never-declared, legally boring, copy rules. None asks for an internal key on screen. The id's only provenance is a card spec wording — docs/ui-transition-plan.md:121 (U-07) says \"each row = name, path, current weight, a slider...\" — an implementation-plan phrase, not a principle.\n\nThe usual defense (paths disambiguate) does not hold. Only two label collisions exist across 194 nodes: `history`/`computing/history` (both \"History\") and `gaming/design`/`culture/design` (both \"Design\"). renderInterests already groups rows under `<h3 class=\"interest-group-label\">${esc(g.root.label)}</h3>` (app.js:556), so the parent is already on screen for both collisions. The path adds nothing the heading does not already give.\n\nNo second surface does this better — `#/interests` (routed at app.js:10635, reached via the drawer link injected by `ensureInterestsDrawerLink`, app.js:9463-9487) is the only place these sliders exist. NOT already_handled.\n\nAlso for the plan: the persona's fallback of \"a human description on the second line\" is NOT available. Zero of the 194 taxonomy nodes carry a description field (keys are id, parent, label, terms, apple_anchor, weight, confidence, last_evidence_at). Authoring 194 descriptions is a far larger job than deleting one span. And taxonomy.json's own `notes` warns node ids are stable identifiers keyed off by seven other data files plus CI — so the fix is to stop DISPLAYING the id, never to rename ids.\n\n(B) \"Reset to learned\" / \"Drag a slider to overrule what 4a has learned\" — DELIBERATE, do not touch. This is CLAUDE.md product principle #2, \"State observed, never declared. No config fields or manual 'done' declarations where observation is possible.\" The weight is an observed value nudged by plays and thumbs (`nudgeTopics`, propagating to the parent at PARENT_NUDGE_RATIO 0.5); the slider is an override laid on top of learning, and the button restores the taxonomy-authored default. \"Reset to learned\" is the accurate name for that and is plain English — not the \"beats\"-style invented jargon this audit was hunting for. Renaming it to \"Reset\" or \"Reset to default\" would misdescribe what the button does and flatten the observed/declared distinction the principle exists to protect. test/interests-page.test.js:335,349 pin this control.\n\nAPPLE COMPARISON — fair, with one qualification. The load-bearing half (\"nothing in a consumer podcast app shows you a database key\") holds, and this repo corroborates it: taxonomy.json's `apple_anchor` values are human category names (\"Science > Physics\", \"Arts > Food\"), i.e. Apple's own taxonomy surface is labels, not keys. Qualification: Apple Podcasts ships no per-topic interest-weight screen at all, so there is no Apple screen to compare feature-for-feature; I could not run Apple Podcasts and am not asserting a screen-level match. Fair as a consumer-app norm, not as a like-for-like screen. It barely matters — the id line is redundant against 4a's own labels regardless of what Apple does.\n\nSEVERITY medium: cosmetic but on every row, and it is the one screen where a newcomer is asked to trust that 4a understands their taste — leaking the schema there undercuts exactly that. No functional harm, and the page sits behind a drawer link in Settings, so it blocks no one.\n\nFIX SHAPE: delete the `.interest-row-path` span at app.js:525 (and the orphaned rule at styles.css:983); leave app.js:536 and app.js:552 exactly as they are. The same two lines also exist in archive/legacy-ui-2026-09/app.js.pre-cutover-2026-09-06:528 — that is an archived snapshot and must NOT be edited."
}

======== journal line 154 | result #70 | phase=Check | label=check:jargon:Forays — the app's whole dif | agent=a74bc3cc89741c564
{
 "verdict": "real_friction",
 "corrected_severity": "medium",
 "reason": "Mostly right, with one sub-claim that must be dropped from the fix plan and one that is the real, fixable defect.\n\nDROP — \"Forays are reachable only from the hamburger drawer\" is FALSE, and \"Forays should be a tab\" is a deliberate founder decision.\n(a) Factually wrong: `foraysForYouHtml()` (app.js:6396-6421) renders a \"Forays for you\" section as the SECOND content block of Home — `renderHomeV2()` (app.js:6491-6501) orders it `homeGreeting() / testTrackNoticeHtml() / jumpBackInV2Html() / foraysForYouHtml() / playlistsForYouHtml() / episodesForYouHtml()`. It is a horizontal scroller of up to four Foray cards carrying SegmentStrips, plus an exploration-floor stretch pick. \"Jump back in\" also surfaces Foray resume rows. Only the *index page* `#/forays` is drawer-only; Forays themselves have the most prominent placement in the app.\n(b) Deliberate: docs/ui-transition-plan.md §1 D3 — \"**Adopt the mockup's four-tab bar**: Home · Search · Create · Library. This reverses #467's menu-page Home, deliberately.\" Recorded again in docs/DECISIONS.md:108-117 and pinned by test/tab-bar.test.js:190-207. D8 adds \"**Foray generation stays out** of the UI for now,\" and D10 notes \"**Library is Joey's #374**.\" A Forays tab was considered and not taken. Asking for one re-litigates a founder decision, not a newcomer bug.\n\nKEEP — the naming collision and the mis-highlight are real, unprincipled, and cheap.\n(a) Two nav systems ship side by side with conflicting labels for the same route. index.html:49-53 gives the drawer five primary destinations (Home / Shows / Playlists / Forays / Up Next); app.js:9232-9251 gives the tab bar four (Home / Search / Create / Library). `#/shows` is literally one destination with two names — drawer \"Shows\" (index.html:50), tab \"Search\" (app.js:9237). Both lists are independently pinned by tests written three days apart: test/home-information-architecture.test.js:288-309 asserts the drawer's exact five \"in the founder's order\" (#467, 2026-09-03), test/tab-bar.test.js:190-207 asserts the tabs \"in the mockup's order\" (D3, 2026-09-06). Neither test knows the other exists.\n(b) Root cause is an unfinished cutover, NOT a product principle. D3's own text (docs/DECISIONS.md:112-113) says \"the drawer and the menu page stay reachable from a **Settings entry**\" — that never happened. index.html:35 still fronts the drawer with a top-bar `#menu-btn` ☰ and the drawer still leads with five destinations before its Settings label (index.html:54-56). The app shipped the new primary nav without demoting the old one. Nothing in CLAUDE.md's principles (exploration floor, no dark patterns, state-observed-never-declared, legally-boring) requires or explains two differently-named navs.\n(c) The mis-highlight is confirmed and bidirectional. `tabForHash` (app.js:9264) maps `#/forays` and `#/foray/…` to \"library\", pinned at test/tab-bar.test.js:237-240. Library is four sections, Saved/Playlists/Up Next/History (app.js:7205-7208) under the subtitle they quote (app.js:7203). `grep -i download app.js` returns only TTS voice-file strings (app.js:10015-10027, 10241), Foray source notes (7948, 7974), and the starred-shows comment at 940. There is no settings screen with a download switch (no `renderSettings` exists). `sw.js` caches the app shell only — no audio, no enclosure URLs — so nothing is offline but the UI. NOT ALREADY HANDLED: I checked every playable surface (epRow, renderEpisode, the Up Next page, Library) and the service worker; there is no hidden menu, long-press or alternate label. NOT MISREAD. WHY IT IS DELIBERATE RATHER THAN AVOIDABLE, and note this is more principle-shaped than the persona guessed: (1) Their auto-download expectation is explicitly refused on principle. app.js:940 on starred shows: \"Deliberately NOT subscribe semantics — no notifications, no auto-download, no algorithmic surfacing — just a lightweight marker ... Same 'state observed, never declared' principle: starring a show changes nothing about what the app recommends or fetches.\" A Settings auto-download switch is precisely the declared-config-instead-of-observation that principle 2 bans. That half of the ask should not be built. (2) Manual per-episode download is NOT banned by principle — CLAUDE.md principle 3 (\"Legally boring\") actually names it as the permitted shape: \"Never rehost/proxy/transform episode audio ... download via original enclosure URLs.\" But on the web client it is technically impossible without breaking that same principle: docs/ios-native-player-gap.md:407 — \"No offline downloads (#29/WP9): browser `fetch()` cannot read CORS-less CDN bytes\" — and :186, \"podcast CDNs send no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` (measured, #20 §3) ... AVPlayer is not a browser origin and ignores CORS entirely — which is ... the precondition for offline downloads (#29/WP9).\" The one web workaround is proxying the bytes, which principle 3 forbids outright. So downloads are native-only by construction, filed as issue #29/WP9, and the repo states the gap in its own words at docs/mobile-shell.md:509-511: \"the *curation* is offline-complete and shallower, the *audio* is not offline at all, and #29 (downloads) is the issue that changes the second one.\" docs/narrator-pipeline.md:446-449 adds the only exception — 4a's own TTS narration, whose CORS headers are ours, \"is therefore the first asset #29 could download on *web* as well as native.\" A plan that adds a download cloud to `epRow` on web would ship a button that cannot work; that is the trap to avoid. WHAT SURVIVES AS THE KEPT FINDING, reframed as explanation: the app never tells a listener that audio is streaming-only. Connectivity appears nowhere until it has already failed — FY_START_FAILED (app.js:8526) \"That segment wouldn't load. Check the connection, then press play.\" and app.js:11360 \"Couldn't load 4a — check your connection and reload.\" The ★ Saved list is a bookmark whose name reads like a stored file, so a ten-year Apple Podcasts user reasonably assumes it is one and finds out at 30,000 feet. That is the avoidable part: honest advance copy near Saved and at the point of play about streaming from the publisher's feed, sized so it cannot be mistaken for a promise that downloads are coming. APPLE COMPARISON IS FAIR — Apple Podcasts is download-first: a download control on episode rows, a Downloaded collection in Library, and a per-show/global automatic-download setting for followed shows are all real, long-standing behaviour there, so their expectation is the mainstream norm and not an invented one. Caveat I cannot substantiate from this repo: I did not verify the current iOS version's exact placement (some discovery surfaces put download behind an overflow menu rather than inline), and no file here measures Apple's UI; the claim rests on general knowledge of the app, not on evidence in the codebase. Nothing in 4a's docs disputes it — docs/marketing/01-competitive-landscape.md:127 treats \"the audio-only, eyes-free, offline commute session\" as the category norm 4a is aiming at."
}

Only the index page and the row are mute — fix those, do not rebuild the detail page.\n(b) \"the only explanation in the entire app\" is FALSE. app.js:2792 on the show page: \"Not part of ${show.title}'s own catalogue — 4a stitched a clip from it into these.\" And app.js:6545 already contains the exact sentence that answers \"why so few\" — \"No forays right now — 4a stitches these by hand, so they arrive a few at a time.\" — but it is the EMPTY state, so it is unreachable at N=1, dead precisely when it is needed. That inversion is the single cheapest thing to fix.\n(c) \"worth a tab\" misstates the cost. It is a drawer row (index.html:52), placed there by explicit founder instruction quoted in-code at app.js:6519-6521: \"get rid of the recommended foray at the top of Home. Move it into a page accessible via the menu exclusively for Forays.\" The surface spent is one menu line, not a tab slot.\n\nNOT deliberate_choice: I read all four product principles (CLAUDE.md:236-254). Exploration floor / no-dark-patterns, state-observed-never-declared, and legally-boring do not touch this; copy rule #4 constrains how an explainer is worded (bans \"deep dive\", \"delve\", clickbait withholding) but never forbids one. Nothing in the stated principles produces a mute index page, and the #/forays subtitle slot already exists with room in it.\n\nBUT the COUNT is a documented external constraint, not UX friction — do not plan to publish more Forays. STATE.md:857-880 (\"THE REPO HAS A PUBLISHED FORAY\"): the other seven are unpublished on measurements, \"Rule X1 is unmet and is unmeetable today… X1 needs narration AUDIO; there is none in the repo and voicing is founder spend\", and \"Nobody has listened to it end to end\" (HUMAN-ACTIONS.md #8, still open). The actionable half of \"only one to play\" is therefore honesty about scarcity, not more inventory.\n\nSeverity medium, not high: the feature is reachable, the detail page explains itself well, and the scarcity is spend-gated. The avoidable, cheap, principle-compatible fixes are a durable one-line explainer in the existing #/forays subtitle slot, making the hand-stitched scarcity sentence reachable at N>=1, and not letting a scrim tap permanently destroy the first-run explanation.\n\nApple comparison fair: they explicitly withdrew it (\"Not that Forays should resemble anything in Apple Podcasts — they should not\"), so no claim about Apple's behaviour is being relied on. # 4a — NEWCOMER AUDIT

Six personas produced 83 complaints. I re-verified the load-bearing ones against the source rather than trusting the summaries; corrections to their line numbers and claims are marked inline. Everything below is quoted from files I opened this session. **No files were modified.**

---

## 1. THE FIRST FIVE MINUTES

### 0:00 — a dark rectangle with two glyphs

`init()` fetches `data/session.json` first (`app.js:11357`), then awaits nine more files in one `Promise.all` (`app.js:11379-11394`) before `state.ready = true; route();` at `app.js:11403-11405`. `index.html:61` ships `<main id="view"></main>` empty and nothing writes into it until `route()` runs. I measured the payload on disk: `discover.json` alone is **2457.7 KB**, plus `item-tags.json` 415.7 KB and `segments.json` 200.1 KB — roughly 3.5 MB cold, before one word of product appears. There is no skeleton and no spinner; the only failure copy, `"Couldn't load 4a — check your connection and reload."` (`app.js:11360`), fires only if `session.json` itself fails. Every other slow fetch shows nothing at all.

**This is the first comprehension break, and it happens before comprehension has anything to work on.** The user cannot distinguish loading from broken.

*(Correction to the brief: it claimed ten fetches in one `Promise.all`. It is one fetch then nine. Immaterial to the finding.)*

### 0:30 — the intro sheet, and the button most people press

The first-run sheet is the **only** place in the product that explains its differentiator: `"Forays: one subject, many shows"` and `"We clip the best parts of several podcasts on a subject and stitch them into one seamless listen, with a narrator bridging the gaps."` (`app.js:4117-4119`).

Both buttons are appended in this order: `actions.append(skip, go)` (`app.js:4138`), where `skip` is `"Skip for now"` (`app.js:4132`). The left button, first in the DOM, is the one that sets `cp_intro_dismissed` permanently (`skip.addEventListener("click", dismiss)`, `app.js:4143`). I grepped for a route that re-opens it. There is none.

**This is the second and more damaging break.** From here on, "Foray" appears in the drawer (`index.html:52`), as a Home section heading, and as a lowercase kicker over bare titles — and nothing in the app ever says what one is again. The differentiator becomes a nonsense word by design of the button order.

### 1:00 — Home, which contains no play button

`renderHomeV2()` renders the greeting, test-track notice, "Jump back in", "Forays for you", "Playlists for you", "Episodes for you". On a fresh install, "Jump back in" returns `""` (no history), and it is the only rail that calls `playBtn`. Everything else is a link:

- `epRow` is the only thing in the app with a real play control, and it lives one level down: `${inApp}${starBtn(item.id)}${upNextBtn(item.id)}${unavailable}` (`app.js:6569`).
- The "Episodes for you" cards route to `#/subject/<branch>` — a list page, not an episode.

Sixty seconds in, **every tappable thing on the front door is navigation.** Two taps minimum to hear audio.

### 2:00 — "Forays for you" is a section of one, and it is the wrong one

This is the finding I consider most serious in the entire audit, and I verified it by counting the data myself:

```
capital-types-1     published   items: 22   narration: 0
grilling-history-1  draft       items: 32   narration: 0
grilling-history-2  draft       items: 10   narration: 0
geology-plates-1    draft       items: 19   narration: 0
beyond-the-algorithm…  draft    items: 51   narration: 40
how-ai-actually-gets-built…  draft  items: 50  narration: 39
the-chain-reaction…  draft      items: 49   narration: 38
what-engineers-actually-do…  draft  items: 56  narration: 40
```

**The one Foray a newcomer can play contains zero narration items.** The four that carry narration (38–40 each) are all drafts, invisible without the founder's test-track switch. Ninety seconds after the onboarding sheet promised "a narrator bridging the gaps", the app delivers 22 clips butt-cut together with authored silence between them — and `player/seam-gap.js` says in its own header what that sounds like: *"That does not read as an edit. It reads as a glitch."*

The pitch and the shipped article disagree. No amount of copy fixes this one; it needs a publish.

### 3:00 — the tab bar teaches a map the drawer contradicts

`TAB_ROUTES` (`app.js:9232-9250`, read in full) is Home / **Search** / **Create** / Library. The drawer (`index.html:49-53`) is Home / **Shows** / **Playlists** / **Forays** / Up Next.

- `#/shows` is "Search" downstairs and "Shows" upstairs, and the page's own `<h2>` says "Shows".
- `#/playlists` sits under a tab called "Create".
- **Forays has no tab at all**, and `tabForHash` files `#/forays` under `library` — a page whose four sections are `libSection("Saved") / ("Playlists") / ("Up Next") / ("History")` (`app.js:7205-7208`) under the subtitle `"saved, history, playlists &amp; Up Next"` (`app.js:7203`). The tab lights up for a destination it does not list.

An Apple-trained user believes the bottom bar is the whole app. They will never open the hamburger, and therefore **will never find Forays.**

### 4:00 — the first tap on play, and the silence after it

Two compounding problems:

`bindPlay` does `const ok = await window.ForayPlayer.play(item, ...); if (!ok) { clearQueuePlaybackOrigin(); return; }` — no message, no line, nothing. The Foray page, by contrast, has a live `<p class="fy-error" id="fy-error" role="status" aria-live="polite" hidden>` (`app.js:8122`) and the copy `"That segment wouldn't load. Check the connection, then press play."` (`app.js:8526`). **The showcase surface gets the error handling; the surface a newcomer actually uses gets silence.**

Then, when the episode ends, it ends. See §3.

### Where comprehension actually breaks

In order: **(1)** a blank first second with no signal; **(2)** the Skip button that permanently deletes the only explanation of the product; **(3)** the one playable Foray not doing what the explanation promised; **(4)** two navigation systems with different names. Items 2 and 3 are the pair that matter — together they mean a newcomer meets the differentiator as an undefined word attached to an hour of unexplained audio that does not match its own pitch.

---

## 2. JARGON LEDGER

Every internal word I found in user-visible text. All line numbers below were opened and read this session unless marked **[persona-cited, not re-verified]**.

### The "segment" family — one object, five names

| Quoted string | file:line | Plain English |
|---|---|---|
| `aria-label="Previous segment"` / `"Next segment"` | `app.js:8109`, `app.js:8111` | "Previous clip" / "Next clip" |
| `isForay ? "Previous segment" : …` / `"Next segment"` | `player/client.js:2848-2849` | same |
| `` `${r.playable.length} segment${…}` `` | `app.js:7990` | "22 clips" |
| `${lost} segment${…} can't play — listed below.` | `app.js:8124` | "N clips can't play" |
| `"That segment wouldn't load. Check the connection, then press play."` | `app.js:8526` | "That clip wouldn't load…" |
| `"Jingle between segments"` | `app.js:9391` | "Jingle between clips" |
| `` show: `${show} · part ${index + 1} of ${total}` `` | `player/client.js:903` | "clip N of M" |
| `` ? `part ${Math.min(index, total-1)+1} of ${total}` `` | `player/media-session.js:426` | "clip N of M" |
| `Now on piece ${m.currentIndex + 1} of ${pieces}` | `player/segment-strip.js:422` | "clip N of M" |

The code has already decided the listener word: `forayBeatName()` returns `"this clip"` as its fallback. Nothing else follows it. Note the two counts that appear together genuinely disagree — the header counts bars, the strip counts items, so a listener sees "11 segments" and hears "piece 10 of 56".

| `` `Running order: ${parts.join(" and ")}, ${fmtSpan(m.totalSec)} in all.` `` | `player/segment-strip.js:407` | "56 clips from 5 shows, 1 hour 12 minutes in all." |
| `"Running order: nothing to play."` | `player/segment-strip.js:392` | "Nothing to play yet." |

| `"Barbecue: eight beats of a forty-beat history"` | `data/forays.json:284` | "Barbecue: eight chapters of a much longer history" |
| `"this act"` ×14, `"Act one"` ×1, `"last act"` ×2, `"acts back"` ×1 — **spoken aloud** and printed via `narrationScriptHtml` | `data/forays.json` (18 occurrences of these patterns; the brief claimed 30 across a wider set) | "this part", or the section's own title |

**On "beats" specifically, since you asked:** `grilling-history-2` is `status: "draft"`, so it does not reach an ordinary visitor today — but it renders through the normal path the moment it publishes or anyone uses `?foray=`. Worth knowing before you rule: `docs/curation/grilling-history-assembly.md` records the title as *deliberately* unflattering — the point was to admit the Foray covers 8 of 40 beats rather than overpromise a history of barbecue. **The honesty is right; the word carrying it is the one word a listener cannot decode.** "Eight chapters of a much longer history" keeps the disclosure and loses the jargon.
